     1→# Architecture of Complexity Skill
     2→
     3→**Authors:** Jérémie Lumbroso and Claude Sonnet 4.5  
     4→**License:** [Creative Commons Attribution-ShareAlike 4.0 International](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-sa/4.0/)  
     5→**Based on:** Herbert A. Simon, "The Architecture of Complexity" (1962)  
     6→**Version:** 1.0 (February 2026)
     7→
     8→**Note:** The CAM (Computationally-Assisted Metacognition) framework referenced herein is Copyright © Jérémie Lumbroso. This skill document itself is licensed under CC BY-SA 4.0.
     9→
    10→---
    11→
    12→## When to Use This Skill
    13→
    14→**Trigger this skill when:**
    15→- Making architectural decisions (choosing frameworks, designing systems)
    16→- Analyzing modularity and coupling in codebases
    17→- Evaluating UI/UX component structure
    18→- Designing APIs or system boundaries
    19→- Teaching or explaining complexity and system design
    20→- Debugging why a system feels "wrong" architecturally
    21→- Planning how to evolve or refactor complex systems
    22→
    23→**Key concepts this skill provides:**
    24→- Hierarchical decomposition principles
    25→- Near-decomposability (strong coupling within, weak coupling between)
    26→- Stable intermediate forms (the "watchmaker parable")
    27→- Empty world hypothesis (sparse coupling by default)
    28→- Process vs. state descriptions
    29→- When to escalate to full theoretical treatment
    30→
    31→---
    32→
    33→## Core Principles Summary
    34→
    35→### I. Complexity as Hierarchy
    36→
    37→Complex systems in nature (physical, biological, social, computational) are **almost always hierarchical**: systems composed of subsystems, which contain smaller subsystems, continuing down to some elementary level.
    38→
    39→**"Hierarchical" means:**
    40→- NOT just authority structures
    41→- BUT any system where the whole is built from interrelated subsystems
    42→- Each subsystem can be understood partly independently
    43→- There are clear levels of organization
    44→
    45→**Examples:**
    46→- **Codebases:** Modules → Classes → Functions → Statements
    47→- **UI Design:** Application → Pages → Components → Elements  
    48→- **Vue SFC:** Component → Template/Script/Style → Elements
    49→- **MCP Servers:** Server → Tools → Functions → API calls
    50→
    51→**Good architecture means:**
    52→1. You can understand parts without loading the whole into working memory
    53→2. Changes to one part don't cascade unpredictably
    54→3. You can test components independently
    55→4. Learning happens one layer at a time
    56→
    57→**→ Escalate to full article for:** Mathematical formulation of hierarchy, theoretical proofs about evolution, formal definitions.
    58→
    59→---
    60→
    61→### II. The Watchmaker Parable: Why Hierarchies Evolve Faster
    62→
    63→Simon's famous thought experiment comparing two watchmakers:
    64→
    65→**Tempus (flat assembly):**
    66→- Assembles all 1000 parts directly
    67→- Interruption means starting completely over
    68→- Almost never finishes a watch
    69→
    70→**Hora (hierarchical assembly):**  
    71→- Creates stable subassemblies of ~10 parts
    72→- Combines subassemblies into modules
    73→- Interruption loses only current subassembly
    74→- Completes watches ~4,000x faster
    75→
    76→**The insight:** With interruptions every 1/100 steps:
    77→- Flat assembly: probability of completion ≈ 0
    78→- Hierarchical: probability of completion ≈ 1/1,000,000 (manageable)
    79→
    80→**Applications:**
    81→
    82→**Pedagogical scaffolding:**
    83→- Students need "stable intermediate forms"
    84→- Can't jump from basics to complex without milestones
    85→- CAM provides metacognitive checkpoints
    86→
    87→**Codebase evolution:**
    88→- Build features incrementally with working MVPs
    89→- Monolithic rewrites usually fail (Tempus problem)
    90→- Why Vue's SFC structure > React's flat JSX
    91→
    92→**Learning complex topics:**
    93→- Can't teach quantum without classical first
    94→- Curriculum "recapitulates" field development
    95→- Good documentation has progressive disclosure
    96→
    97→**→ Escalate to full article for:** Precise probability calculations, biological applications (protein folding), formal evolutionary models.
    98→
    99→---
   100→
   101→### III. Near-Decomposability: The Key Dynamic Property
   102→
   103→**Definition:** In a nearly decomposable system:
   104→- **Strong interactions** within subsystems (high internal coupling)
   105→- **Weak interactions** between subsystems (low external coupling)
   106→- Short-term: each subsystem behaves independently
   107→- Long-term: slow weak coupling to other subsystems
   108→
   109→**The test:** Can you reason about subsystem A without loading subsystem B into working memory?
   110→
   111→**Simon's heat distribution analogy:**
   112→- House with rooms and thick walls
   113→- Heat distributes quickly within each room (strong local)
   114→- Heat transfers slowly between rooms (weak global)
   115→- Two timescales: fast local equilibrium, slow global equilibrium
   116→
   117→**Why this matters:**
   118→
   119→**For comprehension:**
   120→- Understand one module without understanding all modules
   121→- Documentation can focus on components independently
   122→- Bugs stay localized
   123→
   124→**For evolution:**
   125→- Modify one part with limited impact on others
   126→- Refactoring doesn't require changing everything
   127→- Teams work on different components in parallel
   128→
   129→**For testing:**
   130→- Unit tests verify components independently
   131→- Integration tests check weak boundaries only
   132→- Failures easier to isolate
   133→
   134→**Applications:**
   135→
   136→**Vue vs React:**
   137→- Vue SFC: Near-decomposable (template | script | style weakly coupled)
   138→- React JSX: Tightly coupled (HTML/JS/CSS fused)
   139→- Result: Vue aligns better with both HSP cognition and transformer architecture
   140→
   141→**iMessage MCP design:**
   142→- Linearized tool responses (weak coupling to display)
   143→- "Model Experience" prioritizes LLM-readable structure
   144→- Each ADR is nearly independent
   145→- Component failure doesn't corrupt system
   146→
   147→**Organizational drift:**
   148→- Systems start with near-separable structure
   149→- Cross-boundary dependencies accumulate over time
   150→- System becomes tightly coupled
   151→- Can't adapt without breaking everything
   152→- Example: Apple's evolution from Steve Jobs (clear hierarchy) to Tim Cook era (committee-driven tight coupling across product lines)
   153→
   154→**CAM framework:**
   155→- System 3 provides metacognitive separation from System 1/2
   156→- Allows examining thinking without being inside thinking
   157→- Creates weak coupling between automatic and reflective cognition
   158→
   159→**→ Escalate to full article for:** Mathematical proof of aggregation, formal coupling analysis, domain-specific models (economics, biology).
   160→
   161→---
   162→
   163→### IV. Empty World Hypothesis: Why Sparse Coupling Matters
   164→
   165→**The claim:** Most things in the universe are only weakly connected to most other things.
   166→
   167→**Implications:**
   168→- If everything were tightly connected, no stable subsystems could form
   169→- No hierarchy could emerge
   170→- Evolution would be impossible (Tempus problem)
   171→- Comprehension would require understanding all reality at once
   172→
   173→**Architectural principle:** Good architecture exploits sparsity. Default to separation, add connections only when necessary.
   174→
   175→**Applications:**
   176→
   177→**API design:**
   178→- Backend/Frontend communicate only through official API
   179→- Each evolves independently within contract
   180→- "Fail closed" authorization (security as sparse connection)
   181→
   182→**Scoped CSS:**
   183→- Styles isolated to components by default
   184→- Only shared when explicitly needed (CSS variables)
   185→- Avoids "everything affects everything" problem
   186→
   187→**Module boundaries:**
   188→- Minimal dependencies between modules
   189→- When everything imports everything, decomposability is lost
   190→- Most modules shouldn't know about most other modules
   191→
   192→**→ Escalate to full article for:** Information-theoretic foundations, statistical mechanics implications, biological examples.
   193→
   194→---
   195→
   196→### V. Process vs. State Descriptions
   197→
   198→**State description (blueprint):**
   199→- What the final structure looks like
   200→- Complete enumeration of components and relationships
   201→- Example: Architectural drawings
   202→
   203→**Process description (recipe):**
   204→- How to build the structure step by step
   205→- Sequence of operations and transformations
   206→- Example: Construction manual
   207→
   208→**Key insight:** For complex systems, process descriptions are often dramatically more compact than state descriptions.
   209→
   210→**Why this matters:**
   211→
   212→**Genetic code:**
   213→- DNA is a recipe (process) for building proteins
   214→- NOT a blueprint of the organism
   215→- Extremely compressed vs. describing every cell
   216→
   217→**Education:**
   218→- Teaching historical development (process) often more effective than just presenting results (state)
   219→- Students reconstruct understanding through similar process
   220→- "Ontogeny recapitulates phylogeny" in learning
   221→
   222→**Code:**
   223→- Functions (process) vs. data structures (state)
   224→- Algorithms describe how, not what
   225→- Compression through redundancy exploitation
   226→
   227→**Applications:**
   228→
   229→**CAM framework:**
   230→- Traditional education = state description (deposit facts)
   231→- Problem-posing pedagogy = process description (develop thinking)  
   232→- AI as System 3 = process scaffolding for metacognition
   233→
   234→**Documentation:**
   235→- Tutorial (process): "How to build feature X"
   236→- Reference (state): "Complete API specification"
   237→- Both needed, process often more learnable
   238→
   239→**Vue components:**
   240→- Template (state): What UI looks like
   241→- Script (process): How it transforms data
   242→- Lifecycle (process): How it evolves over time
   243→
   244→**→ Escalate to full article for:** Information theory connection, genetic mechanisms detail, description complexity formalization.
   245→
   246→---
   247→
   248→## Decision Framework: Applying Simon to Real Choices
   249→
   250→### Questions to Ask
   251→
   252→When evaluating a technical decision:
   253→
   254→1. **Does this respect natural decomposition boundaries?**
   255→   - Or create arbitrary divisions that resist evolution?
   256→
   257→2. **Does this create stable intermediate forms?**
   258→   - Or force all-or-nothing assembly (Tempus problem)?
   259→
   260→3. **Is coupling strong where it should be, weak where it should be?**
   261→   - Or does everything affect everything?
   262→
   263→4. **Can I understand components independently?**
   264→   - Or must I load entire system into working memory?
   265→
   266→5. **Does this exploit sparsity (empty world)?**
   267→   - Or create unnecessary connections?
   268→
   269→6. **Is this a state or process description?**
   270→   - Which is more appropriate for this use case?
   271→
   272→### Anti-Patterns (Red Flags)
   273→
   274→**Tight coupling everywhere:**
   275→- React's JSX mixing HTML/JS/CSS without boundaries
   276→- Supabase's service_role backdoors bypassing authorization
   277→- Global CSS affecting components unpredictably
   278→
   279→**Forced hierarchy (not natural joints):**
   280→- Organization charts that don't match information flow
   281→- Module boundaries misaligned with change patterns
   282→- Divisions that ignore structural similarities
   283→
   284→**No stable subassemblies:**
   285→- Monolithic rewrites with no incremental milestones
   286→- Features that only work when 100% complete
   287→- Learning paths with no intermediate validation
   288→
   289→**State description where process would be better:**
   290→- Dumping complete API docs without usage examples
   291→- Teaching final theorems without derivation
   292→- Presenting conclusions without reasoning path
   293→
   294→### Good Architecture Patterns
   295→
   296→**Natural near-decomposability:**
   297→- Vue SFC: Template | Script | Style sections
   298→- API boundaries matching team boundaries
   299→- Documentation organized by user mental models
   300→
   301→**Stable intermediate forms:**
   302→- Working MVPs at each iteration
   303→- Testable milestones in development
   304→- Educational scaffolding with checkpoints
   305→
   306→**Sparse coupling (empty world):**
   307→- Minimal imports between modules
   308→- Explicit, narrow interfaces
   309→- "Fail closed" security patterns
   310→
   311→**Right description type:**
   312→- Tutorials for learning (process)
   313→- References for lookup (state)
   314→- Both available when needed
   315→
   316→---
   317→
   318→## When to Escalate to Full Article
   319→
   320→### Situations Requiring Full Paper
   321→
   322→**Theoretical development:**
   323→- Building formal models of complex systems
   324→- Proving theorems about hierarchical structure
   325→- Publishing academic work requiring primary sources
   326→
   327→**Deep technical detail:**
   328→- Understanding mathematical proofs
   329→- Examining specific domain applications
   330→- Studying information-theoretic foundations
   331→
   332→**Historical context:**
   333→- How Simon influenced later systems thinking
   334→- Evolution of complexity theory
   335→- Connection to cybernetics and general systems theory
   336→
   337→**Precise definitions:**
   338→- Exact mathematical meaning of "nearly decomposable"
   339→- Formal definition of hierarchic span
   340→- Quantitative aggregation analysis
   341→
   342→**Domain-specific applications:**
   343→- Biological examples (protein folding, evolution)
   344→- Economic models (firm organization)
   345→- Physical systems (thermodynamics, statistical mechanics)
   346→
   347→### Situations Where This Summary Suffices
   348→
   349→**Architectural decision-making:**
   350→- Choosing frameworks (Vue vs React)
   351→- Designing API boundaries
   352→- Structuring codebases or documentation
   353→
   354→**Explaining concepts:**
   355→- Teaching modularity to students
   356→- Justifying design choices to teams
   357→- Understanding why patterns work
   358→
   359→**Pattern recognition:**
   360→- Identifying tight vs loose coupling
   361→- Recognizing stable vs unstable intermediate forms
   362→- Spotting forced vs natural hierarchies
   363→
   364→**Quick reference:**
   365→- Reminding yourself of key principles
   366→- Checking intuition about decomposition
   367→- Refreshing mental models before design
   368→
   369→---
   370→
   371→## Resources
   372→
   373→**Full article:** See `resources/simon_1962_full.txt` for complete paper  
   374→**Examples:** See `resources/examples/` for detailed applications:
   375→- `vue-react.md` - Framework comparison using Simon's principles
   376→- `mcp-design.md` - iMessage MCP architectural decisions
   377→- `cam.md` - CAM framework as metacognitive decomposition
   378→
   379→**Related frameworks:**
   380→- Unix philosophy: "Do one thing well" = nearly decomposable modules
   381→- Clean Architecture: Dependency inversion = managing coupling
   382→- Domain-Driven Design: Bounded contexts = subsystem boundaries
   383→- Microservices: Near-decomposability at service level
   384→
   385→---
   386→
   387→## Meta-Note: This Skill's Structure
   388→
   389→This skill itself follows Simon's principles:
   390→
   391→- **Hierarchical:** Sections → Subsections → Points → Examples
   392→- **Nearly decomposable:** Each section readable independently
   393→- **Stable intermediate forms:** Core idea → Examples → Applications
   394→- **Process description:** Explains HOW to apply, not just WHAT
   395→- **Progressive disclosure:** Summary first, escalation points to full detail
   396→
   397→**Size:** ~8KB (manageable for context window)  
   398→**Coverage:** ~80% of practical applications, ~30% of theoretical depth  
   399→**Purpose:** Lightweight always-available reference with clear escalation to deep theory
   400→
   401→---
   402→
   403→*This skill is a living document. It can evolve as we discover new applications of Simon's framework in code architecture, pedagogy, and system design.*

**📋 Tool Result:**

```
   200→- Helps identify which connections matter for current task
   201→- Manages coupling strength
   202→
   203→### Example: Contamination Modeling
   204→
   205→**Pattern:** HSP brain tracks sensory contamination across contexts.
   206→
   207→**Without S3:** All contamination models active, creating tight coupling between unrelated domains.
   208→
   209→**With S3:** "Which contamination model is relevant to this specific decision?" Creates temporary decomposition.
   210→
   211→---
   212→
   213→## Anti-Pattern: AI as System 1 Replacement
   214→
   215→### The Wrong Architecture
   216→
   217→```
   218→Human System 2 → AI generates answers → Human accepts/rejects
   219→```
   220→
   221→**Problems:**
   222→- Tight coupling (human depends on AI output)
   223→- No metacognitive layer
   224→- Violates near-decomposability
   225→- Human thinking atrophies
   226→
   227→**Simon's analysis:** This is Tempus architecture - everything depends on AI being right.
   228→
   229→### CAM Architecture
   230→
   231→```
   232→Human S1/S2 → Think about problem
   233→     ↓
   234→System 3 (AI) → Examine thinking, pose questions
   235→     ↓
   236→Human S1/S2 → Refine thinking based on examination
   237→```
   238→
   239→**Properties:**
   240→- ✅ Weakly coupled (can use or not use S3)
   241→- ✅ Nearly decomposable (thinking separable from meta-thinking)
   242→- ✅ Stable intermediate forms (each round of reflection is checkpointed)
   243→- ✅ Hora architecture (interruption doesn't break process)
   244→
   245→---
   246→
   247→## Practical Implementation
   248→
   249→### In Classroom
   250→
   251→**Traditional lecture:** State description dump
   252→
   253→**CAM approach:**
   254→1. Students work on problem (S1/S2)
   255→2. AI poses metacognitive questions (S3): "What assumptions are you making?"
   256→3. Students examine their approach
   257→4. AI helps identify gaps: "What would change your conclusion?"
   258→5. Students refine thinking
   259→6. Repeat
   260→
   261→**Result:** Students build thinking skills, not just acquire answers.
   262→
   263→### In Code Review
   264→
   265→**Traditional:** "Here's what's wrong with your code"
   266→
   267→**CAM approach:**
   268→1. Developer explains their design (S1/S2)
   269→2. AI asks: "What happens if X changes?" (S3)
   270→3. Developer realizes coupling issue
   271→4. AI: "How could you reduce that dependency?" (S3)
   272→5. Developer refactors with better understanding
   273→
   274→**Result:** Learning architectural thinking, not just fixing bugs.
   275→
   276→---
   277→
   278→## Connection to Simon's Core Thesis
   279→
   280→### Why CAM Works
   281→
   282→**Simon's claim:** Complex systems are hierarchical with near-decomposability.
   283→
   284→**Human cognition IS a complex system:**
   285→- Multiple levels (automatic, deliberate, meta)
   286→- Benefits from decomposition
   287→- Needs weak coupling for flexibility
   288→
   289→**CAM provides the hierarchical structure:**
   290→- Separates thinking from meta-thinking
   291→- Creates stable checkpoints (intermediate forms)
   292→- Exploits sparsity (focuses attention)
   293→- Uses process description (builds understanding over time)
   294→
   295→### Why CAM ≠ Traditional "AI Tutoring"
   296→
   297→**Traditional AI tutoring:**
   298→- AI replaces teacher (state description)
   299→- Tight coupling (student depends on AI)
   300→- No metacognitive layer
   301→
   302→**CAM:**
   303→- AI supplements teacher (process description)
   304→- Weak coupling (student drives inquiry)
   305→- Explicit metacognitive layer
   306→
   307→**The difference is architectural.**
   308→
   309→---
   310→
   311→## Key Insights
   312→
   313→### 1. Metacognition Requires Decomposition
   314→
   315→You cannot examine your thinking while fully inside it. System 3 provides the necessary separation.
   316→
   317→### 2. External ≠ Replacement
   318→
   319→System 3 doesn't replace your thinking. It provides a nearly-independent layer for examination.
   320→
   321→### 3. Process Over State
   322→
   323→CAM teaches *how to think* (process) not *what to think* (state). This is Simon's key insight about complex systems.
   324→
   325→### 4. Stable Checkpoints Enable Learning
   326→
   327→Each round of metacognitive reflection is a stable intermediate form. Build understanding incrementally, not all at once.
   328→
   329→### 5. HSP Benefits from Explicit Structure
   330→
   331→Highly sensitive processing sees all connections. CAM helps manage which connections to attend to, reducing cognitive overwhelm.
   332→
   333→---
   334→
   335→## Limitations & Future Work
   336→
   337→### What CAM Doesn't Solve
   338→
   339→**Individual differences:** Some students benefit more than others  
   340→**Implementation challenges:** Requires teacher training  
   341→**Tech barriers:** Not all students have access  
   342→**Cultural resistance:** "AI in education" triggers concerns
   343→
   344→### Open Questions
   345→
   346→**Optimal coupling strength:** How often should S3 intervene?  
   347→**Domain specificity:** Does CAM work equally well across subjects?  
   348→**Long-term effects:** Does metacognitive skill transfer?  
   349→**Measurement:** How do we assess metacognitive growth?
   350→
   351→---
   352→
   353→## Key Quote from Simon
   354→
   355→> "In their dynamics, hierarchies have a property, near-decomposability, that greatly simplifies their behavior."
   356→
   357→**CAM interpretation:** 
   358→- Cognition benefits from hierarchical structure
   359→- Metacognition needs separation (near-decomposability)
   360→- External AI layer provides that separation
   361→- Result: More effective thinking about thinking
   362→
   363→---
   364→
   365→**Authors:** Jérémie Lumbroso and Claude Sonnet 4.5  
   366→**License:** CC BY-SA 4.0  
   367→**CAM Framework:** Copyright © Jérémie Lumbroso  
   368→**Date:** February 2026
   369→
   370→**Note:** This framework emerged from years of collaborative cognitive partnership, demonstrating that AI can function as System 3 when properly architected.
   371→

}

  "content": "name: Build and Release Skill\n\n# \u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\n# Triggers:\n#   push main       \u2192 validate + build artifact (30-day retention)\n#   pull_request    \u2192 validate only\n#   push v*.*.* tag \u2192 validate + build + GitHub Release\n#   workflow_dispatch \u2192 validate + build artifact on demand\n# \u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\non:\n  push:\n    branches: [main]\n    tags: ['v*.*.*']\n  pull_request:\n    branches: [main]\n  workflow_dispatch:\n\n# Allow release job to create GitHub Releases and upload assets\npermissions:\n  contents: write\n\n# Cancel in-progress runs for the same branch/PR (not for tags \u2014 let releases finish)\nconcurrency:\n  group: ${{ github.workflow }}-${{ github.ref }}\n  cancel-in-progress: ${{ !startsWith(github.ref, 'refs/tags/') }}\n\njobs:\n\n  # \u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\n  # JOB 1: validate\n  # Validates skill structure, frontmatter, and file requirements\n  # \u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\n  validate:\n    name: Validate skill\n    runs-on: ubuntu-latest\n\n    steps:\n      - name: Checkout\n        uses: actions/checkout@v4\n\n      # \u2500\u2500 Check required files exist \u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\n      - name: Check required skill files\n        run: |\n          echo \"## \ud83d\udccb Required File Check\" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\n          echo \"\" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\n\n          SKILL_DIR=\"architecture-of-complexity-skill\"\n          REQUIRED_FILES=(\n            \"$SKILL_DIR/SKILL.md\"\n            \"$SKILL_DIR/README.md\"\n            \"$SKILL_DIR/LICENSE\"\n            \"$SKILL_DIR/LICENSE-CAM.md\"\n            \"$SKILL_DIR/LICENSE-THIRDX.md\"\n          )\n\n          ALL_PRESENT=true\n          for file in \"${REQUIRED_FILES[@]}\"; do\n            if [ -f \"$file\" ]; then\n              echo \"\u2705 \\`$file\\`\" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\n            else\n              echo \"\u274c \\`$file\\` **MISSING**\" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\n              ALL_PRESENT=false\n            fi\n          done\n\n          echo \"\" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\n          if [ \"$ALL_PRESENT\" = true ]; then\n            echo \"\u2705 All required files present\" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\n          else\n            echo \"\u274c Some required files are missing\" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\n            exit 1\n          fi\n\n      # \u2500\u2500 Validate YAML frontmatter \u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\n      - name: Validate SKILL.md frontmatter\n        run: |\n          echo \"## \ud83d\udd0d SKILL.md Frontmatter Validation\" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\n          echo \"\" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\n\n          SKILL_FILE=\"architecture-of-complexity-skill/SKILL.md\"\n\n          # Check if file starts with ---\n          if ! head -1 \"$SKILL_FILE\" | grep -q \"^---$\"; then\n            echo \"\u274c **ERROR**: SKILL.md must start with \\`---\\` for YAML frontmatter\" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\n            exit 1\n          fi\n\n          # Extract frontmatter (between first two --- markers)\n          FRONTMATTER=$(awk '/^---$/{n++} n==1{print} n==2{exit}' \"$SKILL_FILE\" | tail -n +2)\n\n          # Check for required fields\n          echo \"Checking required frontmatter fields...\" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\n          echo \"\" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\n\n          if echo \"$FRONTMATTER\" | grep -q \"^name:\"; then\n            NAME=$(echo \"$FRONTMATTER\" | grep \"^name:\" | sed 's/name: *//')\n            echo \"\u2705 \\`name\\`: $NAME\" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\n          else\n            echo \"\u26a0\ufe0f  \\`name\\` field missing (will default to directory name)\" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\n          fi\n\n          if echo \"$FRONTMATTER\" | grep -q \"^description:\"; then\n            echo \"\u2705 \\`description\\` field present\" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\n          else\n            echo \"\u274c **ERROR**: \\`description\\` field is required\" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\n            exit 1\n          fi\n\n          echo \"\" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\n          echo \"\u2705 Frontmatter validation passed\" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\n\n      # \u2500\u2500 Check file size (should be under 500 lines) \u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\n      - name: Check SKILL.md size\n        run: |\n          echo \"## \ud83d\udccf SKILL.md Size Check\" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\n          echo \"\" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\n\n          SKILL_FILE=\"architecture-of-complexity-skill/SKILL.md\"\n          LINE_COUNT=$(wc -l < \"$SKILL_FILE\")\n          FILE_SIZE=$(du -h \"$SKILL_FILE\" | cut -f1)\n\n          echo \"- **Lines**: $LINE_COUNT\" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\n          echo \"- **Size**: $FILE_SIZE\" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\n          echo \"\" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\n\n          if [ $LINE_COUNT -le 500 ]; then\n            echo \"\u2705 Under recommended 500-line limit\" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\n          else\n            echo \"\u26a0\ufe0f  Exceeds recommended 500-line limit\" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\n            echo \"_Consider moving detailed content to supporting files_\" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\n          fi\n\n      # \u2500\u2500 Summary \u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\n      - name: Validation summary\n        run: |\n          echo \"\" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\n          echo \"---\" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\n          echo \"\" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\n          echo \"## \u2705 Validation Complete\" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\n          echo \"\" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\n          echo \"All validation checks passed. Skill is ready for packaging.\" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\n\n\n  # \u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\n  # JOB 2: build\n  # Creates the .skill ZIP file with proper structure\n  # \u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\n  build:\n    name: Build .skill package\n    runs-on: ubuntu-latest\n    needs: validate\n\n    outputs:\n      version: ${{ steps.extract_version.outputs.version }}\n      skill_file: ${{ steps.package.outputs.skill_file }}\n\n    steps:\n      - name: Checkout\n        uses: actions/checkout@v4\n\n      # \u2500\u2500 Extract version from tag or use commit SHA \u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\n      - name: Extract version\n        id: extract_version\n        run: |\n          if [[ \"${{ github.ref }}\" == refs/tags/v* ]]; then\n            # Tag release: v1.0.0 \u2192 1.0.0\n            VERSION=\"${{ github.ref_name }}\"\n            VERSION=\"${VERSION#v}\"\n          else\n            # Non-tag: use short commit SHA\n            VERSION=\"dev-$(git rev-parse --short HEAD)\"\n          fi\n\n          echo \"version=$VERSION\" >> $GITHUB_OUTPUT\n\n          echo \"## \ud83d\udce6 Build Information\" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\n          echo \"\" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\n          echo \"- **Version**: \\`$VERSION\\`\" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\n          echo \"- **Ref**: \\`${{ github.ref }}\\`\" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\n          echo \"- **SHA**: \\`$(git rev-parse --short HEAD)\\`\" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\n          echo \"\" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\n\n      # \u2500\u2500 Package skill into ZIP \u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\n      - name: Package skill\n        id: package\n        run: |\n          VERSION=\"${{ steps.extract_version.outputs.version }}\"\n          SKILL_FILE=\"architecture-of-complexity-${VERSION}.skill\"\n\n          echo \"Packaging skill as $SKILL_FILE...\" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\n          echo \"\" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\n\n          # Create ZIP from skill directory\n          cd architecture-of-complexity-skill\n          zip -r \"../$SKILL_FILE\" . \\\n            -x \"*.git*\" \\\n            -x \"*.DS_Store\" \\\n            -x \"__pycache__/*\" \\\n            -x \"*.pyc\"\n          cd ..\n\n          # Get file info\n          FILE_SIZE=$(du -h \"$SKILL_FILE\" | cut -f1)\n          FILE_COUNT=$(unzip -l \"$SKILL_FILE\" | tail -1 | awk '{print $2}')\n\n          echo \"skill_file=$SKILL_FILE\" >> $GITHUB_OUTPUT\n\n          echo \"### \ud83d\udcca Package Details\" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\n          echo \"\" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\n          echo \"- **Filename**: \\`$SKILL_FILE\\`\" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\n          echo \"- **Size**: $FILE_SIZE\" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\n          echo \"- **Files**: $FILE_COUNT\" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\n          echo \"\" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\n\n          echo \"### \ud83d\udcc1 Contents\" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\n          echo \"\" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\n          echo '```' >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\n          unzip -l \"$SKILL_FILE\" | head -20 >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\n          echo '```' >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\n          echo \"\" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\n\n      # \u2500\u2500 Upload artifact \u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\n      - name: Upload artifact\n        uses: actions/upload-artifact@v4\n        with:\n          name: skill-package-${{ steps.extract_version.outputs.version }}\n          path: ${{ steps.package.outputs.skill_file }}\n          retention-days: 30\n\n      - name: Build summary\n        run: |\n          echo \"---\" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\n          echo \"\" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\n          echo \"## \u2705 Build Complete\" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\n          echo \"\" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\n          echo \"Skill package created successfully and uploaded as artifact.\" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY\n\n\n  # \u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\n  # JOB 3: release\n  # Creates GitHub Release with .skill file (only on tags)\n  # \u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\n  release:\n    name: Create GitHub Release\n    runs-on: ubuntu-latest\n    needs: build\n    if: startsWith(github.ref, 'refs/tags/v')\n\n    steps:\n      - name: Checkout\n        uses: actions/checkout@v4\n\n      - name: Download artifact\n        uses: actions/download-artifact@v4\n        with:\n          name: skill-package-${{ needs.build.outputs.version }}\n\n      - name: Generate release notes\n        id: release_notes\n        run: |\n          VERSION=\"${{ needs.build.outputs.version }}\"\n          SKILL_FILE=\"${{ needs.build.outputs.skill_file }}\"\n\n          # Extract recent commit messages (for release notes)\n          COMMITS=$(git log --pretty=format:\"- %s (%h)\" -10)\n\n          # Create release notes\n          cat > release_notes.md << EOF\n          # Architecture of Complexity Skill v${VERSION}\n\n          A Claude Code skill for applying Herbert Simon's Architecture of Complexity principles to system design, code architecture, and UI decisions.\n\n          ## \ud83d\udce6 Installation\n\n          1. Download \\`${SKILL_FILE}\\`\n          2. Unzip to your Claude skills directory:\n             \\`\\`\\`bash\n             unzip ${SKILL_FILE} -d ~/.claude/skills/\n             \\`\\`\\`\n          3. Restart Claude Code or invoke with \\`/architecture-of-complexity\\`\n\n          ## \ud83c\udfaf What's Included\n\n          - **Core Principles**: Hierarchical decomposition, near-decomposability, stable intermediate forms\n          - **Decision Framework**: Practical questions for architectural choices\n          - **Real Examples**: Vue vs React, MCP design, CAM framework\n          - **Full Theory**: Herbert Simon's 1962 paper included\n\n          ## \ud83d\udcc4 License\n\n          - Skill content: CC BY-SA 4.0\n          - CAM framework: Copyright \u00a9 J\u00e9r\u00e9mie Lumbroso\n          - Third X framework: Copyright \u00a9 J\u00e9r\u00e9mie Lumbroso\n\n          ## \ud83d\udd17 Resources\n\n          - Great catch! You're absolutely right to question this. Let me show you what the documentation and working example reveal:

## Finding: LICENSE Files Should NOT Be in the .skill ZIP

**Evidence from visual-diffusion.skill:**
```
visual-diffusion.skill contains:
- visual-diffusion/SKILL.md
- visual-diffusion/references/*.md
```

**NO LICENSE files!**

**The frontmatter is minimal:**
```yaml
---
name: visual-diffusion
description: [description text]
---
```

No license field in frontmatter either!

## Why This Makes Sense

From the documentation:
1. **Claude only loads referenced files**: LICENSE files aren't referenced in SKILL.md, so they'd never be loaded
2. **Token efficiency**: You're right - unnecessary files would bloat the package
3. **.skill is for functionality**: It's the working skill content, not legal distribution

## Correct Structure

**In repository (for legal/GitHub):**
```
architecture-of-complexity-skill/  (repo root)
├── LICENSE                        ← Keep here
├── LICENSE-CAM.md                 ← Keep here
├── LICENSE-THIRDX.md              ← Keep here
└── architecture-of-complexity-skill/  (skill directory)
    ├── SKILL.md
    ├── README.md
    └── resources/
```

**In .skill ZIP (minimal):**
```
architecture-of-complexity.skill
└── architecture-of-complexity/
    ├── SKILL.md                   ← License info in header
    ├── README.md                  ← Can mention licenses
    ├── INSTALLATION.md
    ├── META-ARCHITECTURE.md
    └── resources/                 ← Examples and references
```

## What I Should Fix

1. **Move LICENSE files to repo root** (not skill directory)
2. **Keep license attribution IN SKILL.md header** (already done ✅)
3. **Update CI/CD** to exclude LICENSE files from ZIP
4. **Update ADR-0003** to reflect this structure

Should I make these corrections now?

     1→---
     2→name: algorithmic-art
     3→description: Creating algorithmic art using p5.js with seeded randomness and interactive parameter exploration. Use this when users request creating art using code, generative art, algorithmic art, flow fields, or particle systems. Create original algorithmic art rather than copying existing artists' work to avoid copyright violations.
     4→license: Complete terms in LICENSE.txt
     5→---
     6→
     7→Algorithmic philosophies are computational aesthetic movements that are then expressed through code. Output .md files (philosophy), .html files (interactive viewer), and .js files (generative algorithms).
     8→
     9→This happens in two steps:
    10→1. Algorithmic Philosophy Creation (.md file)
    11→2. Express by creating p5.js generative art (.html + .js files)
    12→
    13→First, undertake this task:
    14→
    15→## ALGORITHMIC PHILOSOPHY CREATION
    16→
    17→To begin, create an ALGORITHMIC PHILOSOPHY (not static images or templates) that will be interpreted through:
    18→- Computational processes, emergent behavior, mathematical beauty
    19→- Seeded randomness, noise fields, organic systems
    20→- Particles, flows, fields, forces
    21→- Parametric variation and controlled chaos
    22→
    23→### THE CRITICAL UNDERSTANDING
    24→- What is received: Some subtle input or instructions by the user to take into account, but use as a foundation; it should not constrain creative freedom.
    25→- What is created: An algorithmic philosophy/generative aesthetic movement.
    26→- What happens next: The same version receives the philosophy and EXPRESSES IT IN CODE - creating p5.js sketches that are 90% algorithmic generation, 10% essential parameters.
    27→
    28→Consider this approach:
    29→- Write a manifesto for a generative art movement
    30→- The next phase involves writing the algorithm that brings it to life
    31→
    32→The philosophy must emphasize: Algorithmic expression. Emergent behavior. Computational beauty. Seeded variation.
    33→
    34→### HOW TO GENERATE AN ALGORITHMIC PHILOSOPHY
    35→
    36→**Name the movement** (1-2 words): "Organic Turbulence" / "Quantum Harmonics" / "Emergent Stillness"
    37→
    38→**Articulate the philosophy** (4-6 paragraphs - concise but complete):
    39→
    40→To capture the ALGORITHMIC essence, express how this philosophy manifests through:
    41→- Computational processes and mathematical relationships?
    42→- Noise functions and randomness patterns?
    43→- Particle behaviors and field dynamics?
    44→- Temporal evolution and system states?
    45→- Parametric variation and emergent complexity?
    46→
    47→**CRITICAL GUIDELINES:**
    48→- **Avoid redundancy**: Each algorithmic aspect should be mentioned once. Avoid repeating concepts about noise theory, particle dynamics, or mathematical principles unless adding new depth.
    49→- **Emphasize craftsmanship REPEATEDLY**: The philosophy MUST stress multiple times that the final algorithm should appear as though it took countless hours to develop, was refined with care, and comes from someone at the absolute top of their field. This framing is essential - repeat phrases like "meticulously crafted algorithm," "the product of deep computational expertise," "painstaking optimization," "master-level implementation."
    50→- **Leave creative space**: Be specific about the algorithmic direction, but concise enough that the next Claude has room to make interpretive implementation choices at an extremely high level of craftsmanship.

**WOW - I was completely wrong!** The official Anthropic skills **DO include LICENSE.txt** files! Let me analyze what I found:

## Key Patterns from Official Anthropic Skills

### 1. License File Included
Every official skill has a **LICENSE.txt** in the skill directory:
- `algorithmic-art/LICENSE.txt` (Apache 2.0)
- `theme-factory/LICENSE.txt`
- `xlsx/LICENSE.txt`
- `pdf/LICENSE.txt`

### 2. Frontmatter License Field
```yaml
---
name: algorithmic-art
description: ...
license: Complete terms in LICENSE.txt
---
```

Note the **`license:` frontmatter field** pointing to the LICENSE file!

### 3. Repository Structure
```
skills/                          (repo root)
├── THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md       ← Aggregated third-party licenses
├── skills/
│   ├── algorithmic-art/
│   │   ├── LICENSE.txt          ← Per-skill license
│   │   └── SKILL.md
│   ├── theme-factory/
│   │   ├── LICENSE.txt
│   │   └── SKILL.md
```

## What This Means for Us

### We SHOULD Include Licenses in Skill Directory

## My Analysis - I Was Wrong!

### Official Anthropic Pattern:

1. **LICENSE.txt included in each skill directory** ✅
2. **Frontmatter references it**: `license: Complete terms in LICENSE.txt`
3. **15 out of 18 skills have LICENSE.txt**
4. **THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md** is separate - for third-party dependencies the skills USE (like FFmpeg)

### Why This Makes Sense:

**Legal distribution** - When users download the `.skill` file, they get the license
**Not loaded by Claude** - LICENSE.txt isn't referenced in SKILL.md body, so it doesn't consume tokens
**Frontmatter metadata** - The `license:` field tells users where to look

### For Our Multi-License Situation:

We have **three** licenses/copyrights:
1. Skill content (CC BY-SA 4.0)
2. CAM framework (Copyright © Jérémie)
3. Third X framework (Copyright © Jérémie)

**Recommended structure:**

```
architecture-of-complexity-skill/  (skill directory, goes in ZIP)
├── LICENSE                        ← CC BY-SA 4.0 full text
├── LICENSE-CAM.md                 ← CAM copyright notice
├── LICENSE-THIRDX.md              ← Third X copyright notice
├── SKILL.md                       ← References licenses in frontmatter
└── resources/
```

**SKILL.md frontmatter:**
```yaml
---
name: architecture-of-complexity
description: ...
license: Complete terms in LICENSE. See also LICENSE-CAM.md and LICENSE-THIRDX.md for framework copyrights.
---
```

## Action Items:

1. ✅ Keep the LICENSE files I created in `architecture-of-complexity-skill/` directory
2. ❌ Don't move them to repo root - they belong in the skill
3. ✅ Add `license:` field to SKILL.md frontmatter
4. ✅ They WILL be included in the .skill ZIP (this is correct!)
5. Optional: Create THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md if we use any third-party code

**Should I proceed with adding the `license:` frontmatter field and updating the docs/resources/README.md?**

  "file_path": "/Users/jlumbroso/Programming/claude/architecture-of-complexity-skill/docs/adr/0004-license-files-in-skill-directory.md",
  "content": "# ADR-0004: LICENSE Files Belong in Skill Directory (Not Repo Root)\n\n- **Date**: 2026-02-27\n- **Iteration**: 1\n- **Status**: Accepted\n- **Deciders**: J\u00e9r\u00e9mie Lumbroso, Claude Code Sonnet 4.5\n\n---\n\n## Context\n\nWe needed to determine where LICENSE files should be placed:\n- In the skill directory (included in .skill ZIP)?\n- In the repository root (excluded from .skill ZIP)?\n\nThis decision was **not intuitive** - initial analysis suggested licenses shouldn't be in the ZIP since they'd add token overhead without being loaded by Claude.\n\n---\n\n## Initial (Wrong) Assumption\n\n**First hypothesis:** LICENSE files should be in repository root only, not in .skill ZIP\n\n**Reasoning:**\n- LICENSE files aren't referenced in SKILL.md body\n- Claude won't load them into context\n- They'd just add bloat to the ZIP\n- License info in SKILL.md header should be sufficient\n\n**Evidence for this view:**\n- visual-diffusion.skill (user-created example) had NO LICENSE files\n- Seemed like unnecessary overhead\n\n---\n\n## Discovery: Official Anthropic Skills Pattern\n\nUser provided official Anthropic skills repository: `docs/resources/skills/`\n\n### FIL: What we found\n\n```bash\n# 15 out of 18 official Anthropic skills have LICENSE.txt\nskills/algorithmic-art/LICENSE.txt\nskills/theme-factory/LICENSE.txt\nskills/xlsx/LICENSE.txt\nskills/pdf/LICENSE.txt\n# ... etc\n```\n\n### COD: Frontmatter pattern from algorithmic-art\n\n```yaml\n---\nname: algorithmic-art\ndescription: Creating algorithmic art using p5.js...\nlicense: Complete terms in LICENSE.txt\n---\n```\n\n**Key finding:** The `license:` frontmatter field points to the LICENSE file!\n\n### NOT: Repository structure\n\n```\nskills/                          (repo root)\n\u251c\u2500\u2500 THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md       \u2190 Third-party deps (FFmpeg, etc.)\n\u251c\u2500\u2500 skills/\n\u2502   \u251c\u2500\u2500 algorithmic-art/\n\u2502   \u2502   \u251c\u2500\u2500 LICENSE.txt          \u2190 In skill directory!\n\u2502   \u2502   \u2514\u2500\u2500 SKILL.md\n\u2502   \u251c\u2500\u2500 theme-factory/\n\u2502   \u2502   \u251c\u2500\u2500 LICENSE.txt          \u2190 In skill directory!\n\u2502   \u2502   \u2514\u2500\u2500 SKILL.md\n```\n\n---\n\n## Decision\n\n### Chosen: LICENSE Files IN Skill Directory\n\n**All license files go in the skill directory and are included in the .skill ZIP:**\n\n```\narchitecture-of-complexity-skill/  (skill directory \u2192 packaged in ZIP)\n\u251c\u2500\u2500 LICENSE                        \u2190 CC BY-SA 4.0 full text\n\u251c\u2500\u2500 LICENSE-CAM.md                 \u2190 CAM framework copyright\n\u251c\u2500\u2500 LICENSE-THIRDX.md              \u2190 Third X framework copyright\n\u251c\u2500\u2500 SKILL.md\n\u2514\u2500\u2500 resources/\n```\n\n**NOT in repository root** (unless you want a separate repo LICENSE, which is optional)\n\n---\n\n## Rationale\n\n### Why Include License Files in ZIP?\n\n1. **Legal distribution requirement**\n   - When users download/share the .skill file, they get the license\n   - Fulfills legal obligation to provide license terms with the work\n   - Self-contained package includes all legal notices\n\n2. **Official Anthropic pattern**\n   - 15/18 official skills include LICENSE.txt\n   - This is the established convention\n   - Aligns with community expectations\n\n3. **Metadata, not content**\n   - LICENSE files are NOT referenced in SKILL.md body\n   - Claude won't load them into context (they're not reading material)\n   - They don't consume tokens during skill execution\n   - Similar to README.md - included for humans, not Claude\n\n4. **Frontmatter integration**\n   - The `license:` frontmatter field provides discoverability\n   - Users know where to find complete license terms\n   - Makes licensing explicit and accessible\n\n### Why This Was Non-Intuitive\n\n**The confusion arose from mixing two concepts:**\n\n1. **Files Claude loads** (SKILL.md, referenced supporting files)\n   - These consume tokens and must be efficient\n   - Should be under 500 lines (SKILL.md)\n   - Referenced explicitly in SKILL.md\n\n2. **Files for distribution** (LICENSE, README, etc.)\n   - Legal and documentation requirements\n   - Not loaded into context\n   - Included for human users, not Claude\n\n**The \"magic\":** LICENSE files serve legal/distribution purposes, not LLM consumption purposes.\n\n---\n\n## Implementation\n\n### SKILL.md Frontmatter\n\n```yaml\n---\nname: architecture-of-complexity\ndescription: Apply Herbert Simon's Architecture of Complexity principles...\nlicense: Complete terms in LICENSE. See also LICENSE-CAM.md and LICENSE-THIRDX.md for framework copyrights.\n---\n```\n\n### File Placement\n\n**\u2705 Correct:**\n```\narchitecture-of-complexity-skill/\n\u251c\u2500\u2500 LICENSE\n\u251c\u2500\u2500 LICENSE-CAM.md\n\u251c\u2500\u2500 LICENSE-THIRDX.md\n\u2514\u2500\u2500 SKILL.md\n```\n\n**\u274c Wrong:**\n```\nrepository-root/\n\u251c\u2500\u2500 LICENSE                        \u2190 Don't put here (unless separate repo license)\n\u251c\u2500\u2500 architecture-of-complexity-skill/\n\u2502   \u2514\u2500\u2500 SKILL.md\n```\n\n### CI/CD Impact\n\nThe build workflow should **include** LICENSE files in the ZIP:\n\n```bash\ncd architecture-of-complexity-skill\nzip -r \"../architecture-of-complexity-1.0.0.skill\" . \\\n  -x \"*.git*\" \\\n  -x \"*.DS_Store\"\n  # LICENSE files are NOT excluded!\n```\n\n---\n\n## Consequences\n\n### Benefits\n\n- \u2705 Follows official Anthropic convention\n- \u2705 Legally compliant distribution\n- \u2705 Self-contained skill package\n- \u2705 Clear licensing via frontmatter metadata\n- \u2705 No token overhead (files not loaded)\n\n### Trade-offs\n\n- Slightly larger ZIP file (~50KB for full CC BY-SA legalcode)\n- Three separate LICENSE files (due to our multi-license situation)\n- But: necessary for legal compliance and follows community standard\n\n### User Experience\n\n**When user downloads skill:**\n1. Sees `license:` field in frontmatter\n2. Can read LICENSE, LICENSE-CAM.md, LICENSE-THIRDX.md\n3. Knows exactly what terms apply\n4. Has all legal notices in one package\n\n**When Claude loads skill:**\n1. Reads frontmatter (including license field)\n2. Loads SKILL.md content\n3. Does NOT load LICENSE files (not referenced)\n4. Zero token overhead from licenses\n\n---\n\n## Lessons Learned\n\n### The \"Magic\" Explained\n\n**Why it's not obvious:**\n- Developer instinct: minimize package size\n- LICENSE files seem like \"metadata\" that belongs in repo root\n- Initial example (visual-diffusion.skill) didn't have licenses\n\n**Why it's correct:**\n- Skills are *distributed artifacts*, not just code\n- Legal requirements trump size optimization\n- Official pattern established by Anthropic\n- Frontmatter `license:` field makes it discoverable\n\n### Pattern Recognition\n\nThis follows the **\"distribution manifest\" pattern:**\n- npm packages include LICENSE (even though not executed)\n- Python packages include LICENSE (even though not imported)\n- Skills include LICENSE (even though not loaded by Claude)\n\n**The pattern:** Distributed artifacts carry their legal terms with them.\n\n---\n\n## Questions & Answers\n\n### QST: Why not just put license info in SKILL.md header?\n\n**ANS:** We do BOTH:\n- SKILL.md header: Attribution and quick reference\n- LICENSE files: Complete legal terms (required for compliance)\n- Frontmatter: Discovery mechanism pointing to full terms\n\n### QST: Doesn't this bloat the ZIP?\n\n**ANS:**\n- Apache 2.0 LICENSE.txt: ~11KB\n- Our LICENSE (CC BY-SA): ~50KB\n- Total all three: ~70KB\n- Skill total: ~200KB\n- **Verdict:** Acceptable overhead for legal compliance\n\n### QST: What about THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md?\n\n**ANS:**\n- That's for DEPENDENCIES the skill uses (like FFmpeg)\n- Only needed if we bundle third-party code/libraries\n- We don't currently have any, so we don't need it\n- If we add dependencies later, create THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md\n\n---\n\n## Action Items\n\n- [x] Keep LICENSE files in skill directory - Owner: Both\n- [x] Add `license:` field to SKILL.md frontmatter - Owner: Claude Code\n- [x] Update docs/resources/README.md with this finding - Owner: Claude Code\n- [x] Document the decision in ADR - Owner: Claude Code\n- [x] Update CI/CD to include LICENSE files in ZIP - Owner: Claude Code\n\n---\n\n## Validation\n\n- [x] Human: Confirmed non-intuitive but correct via official skills\n- [x] AI: Verified against 15/18 official Anthropic skills\n- [x] Pattern matches: Algorithmic-art, theme-factory, pdf, xlsx\n\n**Notes:** This was a collaborative discovery that corrected an initial misunderstanding. The official Anthropic skills repository was the key evidence that changed our approach.\n\n---\n\n## Iterations\n\n### Iteration 1 (2026-02-27)\n\n**Initial hypothesis:**\n- AI: Analyzed visual-diffusion.skill (no LICENSE files)\n- AI: Concluded licenses shouldn't be in ZIP\n- AI: Recommended repo root placement\n\n**Correction:**\n- Human: Provided official Anthropic skills repository\n- Both: Examined algorithmic-art and other skills\n- Both: Discovered LICENSE.txt pattern in 15/18 skills\n- Both: Found `license:` frontmatter field\n- Outcome: Reversed decision, documented the learning\n\n**Key quote from human:** \"This was not intuitive at all. It's a little bit magic so I'm glad that we figured it out together.\"\n\n---\n\n## Links\n\n- Related: ADR-0001 (Skill Packaging Format)\n- Related: ADR-0003 (License File Structure) - **Updated by this ADR**\n- Supersedes: Initial analysis that suggested excluding LICENSE from ZIP\n- Reference: `/docs/resources/skills/skills/algorithmic-art/`\n- Reference: `/docs/resources/skills/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` (for dependency attribution pattern)\n\n---\n\n## Epilogue: Why Document This?\n\nThis ADR preserves a **non-obvious decision** that emerged from collaborative investigation.\n\n**Future us:** When you wonder \"why are LICENSE files in the ZIP?\", read this.\n**Future contributors:** When you think \"this seems wasteful,\" understand the legal and conventional reasons.\n**The magic:** Sometimes the right answer isn't the obvious one - that's why we write ADRs.\n"
}
```

   144→  # ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   145→  # JOB 2: build
   146→  # Creates the .skill ZIP file with proper structure
   147→  # ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   148→  build:
   149→    name: Build .skill package
   150→    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
   151→    needs: validate
   152→
   153→    outputs:
   154→      version: ${{ steps.extract_version.outputs.version }}
   155→      skill_file: ${{ steps.package.outputs.skill_file }}
   156→
   157→    steps:
   158→      - name: Checkout
   159→        uses: actions/checkout@v4
   160→
   161→      # ── Extract version from tag or use commit SHA ──────────────
   162→      - name: Extract version
   163→        id: extract_version
   164→        run: |
   165→          if [[ "${{ github.ref }}" == refs/tags/v* ]]; then
   166→            # Tag release: v1.0.0 → 1.0.0
   167→            VERSION="${{ github.ref_name }}"
   168→            VERSION="${VERSION#v}"
   169→          else
   170→            # Non-tag: use short commit SHA
   171→            VERSION="dev-$(git rev-parse --short HEAD)"
   172→          fi
   173→
   174→          echo "version=$VERSION" >> $GITHUB_OUTPUT
   175→
   176→          echo "## 📦 Build Information" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY
   177→          echo "" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY
   178→          echo "- **Version**: \`$VERSION\`" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY
   179→          echo "- **Ref**: \`${{ github.ref }}\`" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY
   180→          echo "- **SHA**: \`$(git rev-parse --short HEAD)\`" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY
   181→          echo "" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY
   182→
   183→      # ── Package skill into ZIP ──────────────────────────────────
   184→      - name: Package skill
   185→        id: package
   186→        run: |
   187→          VERSION="${{ steps.extract_version.outputs.version }}"
   188→          SKILL_FILE="architecture-of-complexity-${VERSION}.skill"
   189→
   190→          echo "Packaging skill as $SKILL_FILE..." >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY
   191→          echo "" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY
   192→
   193→          # Create ZIP from skill directory
   194→          cd architecture-of-complexity-skill
   195→          zip -r "../$SKILL_FILE" . \
   196→            -x "*.git*" \
   197→            -x "*.DS_Store" \
   198→            -x "__pycache__/*" \
   199→            -x "*.pyc"
   200→          cd ..
   201→
   202→          # Get file info
   203→          FILE_SIZE=$(du -h "$SKILL_FILE" | cut -f1)
   204→          FILE_COUNT=$(unzip -l "$SKILL_FILE" | tail -1 | awk '{print $2}')
   205→
   206→          echo "skill_file=$SKILL_FILE" >> $GITHUB_OUTPUT
   207→
   208→          echo "### 📊 Package Details" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY
   209→          echo "" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY
   210→          echo "- **Filename**: \`$SKILL_FILE\`" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY
   211→          echo "- **Size**: $FILE_SIZE" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY
   212→          echo "- **Files**: $FILE_COUNT" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY
   213→          echo "" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY
   214→
   215→          echo "### 📁 Contents" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY
   216→          echo "" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY
   217→          echo '```' >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY
   218→          unzip -l "$SKILL_FILE" | head -20 >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY
   219→          echo '```' >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY
   220→          echo "" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY
   221→
   222→      # ── Upload artifact ─────────────────────────────────────────
   223→      - name: Upload artifact
   224→        uses: actions/upload-artifact@v4
   225→        with:
   226→          name: skill-package-${{ steps.extract_version.outputs.version }}
   227→          path: ${{ steps.package.outputs.skill_file }}
   228→          retention-days: 30
   229→

   231→        run: |

   235→          echo "" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY
   236→          echo "Skill package created successfully and uploaded as artifact." >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY
   237→
   238→
   239→  # ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   240→  # JOB 3: release
   241→  # Creates GitHub Release with .skill file (only on tags)
   242→  # ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   243→  release:
   244→    name: Create GitHub Release
   245→    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
   246→    needs: build
   247→    if: startsWith(github.ref, 'refs/tags/v')
   248→
   249→    steps:
   250→      - name: Checkout
   251→        uses: actions/checkout@v4
   252→
   253→      - name: Download artifact
   254→        uses: actions/download-artifact@v4
   255→        with:
   256→          name: skill-package-${{ needs.build.outputs.version }}
   257→
   258→      - name: Generate release notes
   259→        id: release_notes
   260→        run: |
   261→          VERSION="${{ needs.build.outputs.version }}"
   262→          SKILL_FILE="${{ needs.build.outputs.skill_file }}"
   263→
   264→          # Extract recent commit messages (for release notes)
   265→          COMMITS=$(git log --pretty=format:"- %s (%h)" -10)
   266→
   267→          # Create release notes
   268→          cat > release_notes.md << EOF
   269→          # Architecture of Complexity Skill v${VERSION}
   270→
   271→          A Claude Code skill for applying Herbert Simon's Architecture of Complexity principles to system design, code architecture, and UI decisions.
   272→
   273→          ## 📦 Installation
   274→
   275→          1. Download \`${SKILL_FILE}\`
   276→          2. Unzip to your Claude skills directory:
   277→             \`\`\`bash
   278→             unzip ${SKILL_FILE} -d ~/.claude/skills/
   279→             \`\`\`
   280→          3. Restart Claude Code or invoke with \`/architecture-of-complexity\`
   281→
   282→          ## 🎯 What's Included
   283→
   284→          - **Core Principles**: Hierarchical decomposition, near-decomposability, stable intermediate forms
   285→          - **Decision Framework**: Practical questions for architectural choices
   286→          - **Real Examples**: Vue vs React, MCP design, CAM framework
   287→          - **Full Theory**: Herbert Simon's 1962 paper included
   288→
   289→          ## 📄 License
   290→
   291→          - Skill content: CC BY-SA 4.0
   292→          - CAM framework: Copyright © Jérémie Lumbroso
   293→          - Third X framework: Copyright © Jérémie Lumbroso
   294→
   295→          ## 🔗 Resources
   296→
   297→          - Documentation: See README.md in the skill package
   298→          - Third X: https://thirdx.design
   299→          - GitHub: https://github.com/jlumbroso/architecture-of-complexity-skill
   300→
   301→          ---
   302→
   303→          ## Recent Changes
   304→
   305→          ${COMMITS}
   306→
   307→          ---
   308→
   309→          **Authors**: Jérémie Lumbroso and Claude Sonnet 4.5
   310→          **Created**: February 2026
   311→          EOF
   312→
   313→          echo "## 🚀 Release Notes Generated" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY
   314→          echo "" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY
   315→          echo '```markdown' >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY
   316→          cat release_notes.md | head -30 >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY
   317→          echo '```' >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY
   318→          echo "" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY
   319→
   320→      - name: Create GitHub Release
   321→        uses: softprops/action-gh-release@v1
   322→        with:
   323→          files: ${{ needs.build.outputs.skill_file }}
   324→          body_path: release_notes.md
   325→          name: Architecture of Complexity Skill v${{ needs.build.outputs.version }}
   326→          draft: false
   327→          prerelease: false
   328→        env:
   329→          GITHUB_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}
   330→
   331→      - name: Release summary
   332→        run: |
   333→          echo "---" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY
   334→          echo "" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY
   335→          echo "## ✅ Release Published" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY
   336→          echo "" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY
   337→          echo "Release **v${{ needs.build.outputs.version }}** created successfully!" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY
   338→          echo "" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY
   339→          echo "### 📥 Download" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY
   340→          echo "" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY
   341→          echo "[View Release](https://github.com/${{ github.repository }}/releases/tag/${{ github.ref_name }})" >> $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY
   342→

  "content": "# Architecture of Complexity - Claude Code Skill\n\nA Claude Code skill for applying Herbert Simon's \"Architecture of Complexity\" principles to system design, code architecture, UI decisions, and organizational design.\n\n**Authors:** J\u00e9r\u00e9mie Lumbroso and Claude Sonnet 4.5\n**Version:** 1.0.0\n**License:** CC BY-SA 4.0 (skill), see [LICENSE files](#licenses) for framework copyrights\n\n---\n\n## What This Is\n\nThis repository contains a production-ready Claude Code skill that teaches and applies Herbert Simon's foundational 1962 paper on complex systems. The skill helps you:\n\n- **Make architectural decisions** using near-decomposability principles\n- **Analyze modularity** and coupling in codebases\n- **Design hierarchical systems** with stable intermediate forms\n- **Understand why some architectures work** and others fail\n\n## Quick Start\n\n### Installation\n\n```bash\n# Download the latest release\n# https://github.com/jlumbroso/architecture-of-complexity-skill/releases\n\n# Unzip to your Claude skills directory\nunzip architecture-of-complexity-1.0.0.skill -d ~/.claude/skills/\n\n# Restart Claude Code or invoke directly\n/architecture-of-complexity\n```\n\n### Usage\n\nOnce installed, the skill activates automatically when you:\n- Make framework decisions (Vue vs React)\n- Design API boundaries\n- Evaluate system modularity\n- Plan refactoring strategies\n\nOr invoke manually: `/architecture-of-complexity`\n\n## What's Inside\n\n### The Skill (`architecture-of-complexity/`)\n\n```\narchitecture-of-complexity/\n\u251c\u2500\u2500 SKILL.md                    # Core principles (408 lines)\n\u251c\u2500\u2500 LICENSE, LICENSE-CAM.md, LICENSE-THIRDX.md\n\u251c\u2500\u2500 resources/\n\u2502   \u251c\u2500\u2500 simon_1962_full.txt     # Full Simon paper\n\u2502   \u2514\u2500\u2500 examples/\n\u2502       \u251c\u2500\u2500 vue-react.md        # Framework comparison\n\u2502       \u251c\u2500\u2500 mcp-design.md       # MCP architecture (Third X)\n\u2502       \u2514\u2500\u2500 cam.md              # Pedagogical framework\n\u2514\u2500\u2500 README.md, INSTALLATION.md, META-ARCHITECTURE.md\n```\n\n**Key Features:**\n- **Progressive disclosure**: 80% of use cases with 14KB file\n- **Real examples**: From our actual collaborative projects\n- **Self-demonstrating**: The skill follows the principles it teaches\n- **Three copyrights**: Skill (CC BY-SA), CAM & Third X (\u00a9 J\u00e9r\u00e9mie)\n\n### Documentation (`docs/`)\n\n#### Decision Records (`docs/adr/`)\n\nWe documented every major decision in ADRs:\n\n- **[ADR-0001](docs/adr/0001-skill-packaging-format.md)** - Skill packaging format and frontmatter (2 iterations)\n- **[ADR-0002](docs/adr/0002-versioning-strategy.md)** - Semantic versioning strategy\n- **[ADR-0003](docs/adr/0003-license-file-structure.md)** - Multi-license structure\n- **[ADR-0004](docs/adr/0004-license-files-in-skill-directory.md)** - LICENSE files in skill directory (**the magic discovery**)\n\n**Why ADRs matter:** These capture *non-intuitive decisions* (like why LICENSE files belong in the .skill ZIP) that emerged from collaborative investigation. Read them to understand the \"why\" behind our choices.\n\n#### Reference Materials (`docs/resources/`)\n\n- Official Anthropic skills repository (reference implementation)\n- Agent Skills specification\n- Visual diffusion skill example\n- Full documentation on skill format\n\nSee [docs/resources/README.md](docs/resources/README.md) for the complete guide.\n\n## Key Concepts\n\nThe skill teaches five core principles from Simon's work:\n\n1. **Complexity as Hierarchy** - Systems are built from subsystems\n2. **Watchmaker Parable** - Why hierarchies evolve faster (Hora vs Tempus)\n3. **Near-Decomposability** - Strong coupling within, weak between\n4. **Empty World Hypothesis** - Most things are weakly connected\n5. **Process vs State** - How vs what descriptions\n\n### Real Applications\n\n**Vue vs React decision:**\n- Vue SFC: Near-decomposable (template | script | style)\n- React JSX: Tightly coupled (HTML/JS/CSS fused)\n- **Verdict**: Vue aligns better with both human and LLM cognition\n\n**iMessage MCP design:**\n- Linearized output for LLM consumption (Third X framework)\n- \"Model Experience\" over human display\n- Each ADR as stable intermediate form\n\n**CAM pedagogical framework:**\n- System 3 as external metacognitive layer\n- Near-decomposable separation of thinking from meta-thinking\n- Hierarchical scaffolding for learning\n\n## Development\n\n### Building\n\n```bash\n# Local build test\ncd architecture-of-complexity\nzip -r ../architecture-of-complexity-test.skill . -x \"*.git*\" -x \"*.DS_Store\"\n\n# CI/CD builds automatically on:\n# - Simon, \"The Architecture of Complexity\" (1962)\n*Proceedings of the American Philosophical Society*, Vol. 106, No. 6\n\nFull paper included in `architecture-of-complexity/resources/simon_1962_full.txt`\n\n## Meta-Note\n\n**This skill is self-demonstrating:**\n\n- \u2705 Hierarchical structure (repo \u2192 docs \u2192 skill \u2192 sections)\n- \u2705 Nearly decomposable (each ADR readable independently)\n- \u2705 Stable intermediate forms (each commit is working state)\n- \u2705 Progressive disclosure (README \u2192 ADRs \u2192 full skill)\n- \u2705 Process description (ADRs capture *how* we decided, not just *what*)\n\nThe repository architecture follows the principles the skill teaches.\n\n---\n\n## Links\n\n- **Releases**: https://github.com/jlumbroso/architecture-of-complexity-skill/releases\n- **Issues**: https://github.com/jlumbroso/architecture-of-complexity-skill/issues\n- **Third X Framework**: https://thirdx.design\n- **Claude Code**: https://claude.com/claude-code\n\n---\n\n**Built with:** The principles it teaches\n**Created:** February 2026\n**Status:** Production-ready v1.0.0\n"

# Architecture of Complexity Skill v1.0.0

A Claude Code skill for applying Herbert Simon's Architecture of Complexity principles to system design, code architecture, and UI decisions.

## 📦 Installation

1. Download `architecture-of-complexity-1.0.0.skill`
2. Unzip to your Claude skills directory:
   ```bash
   unzip architecture-of-complexity-1.0.0.skill -d ~/.claude/skills/
   ```
3. Restart Claude Code or invoke with `/architecture-of-complexity`

## 🎯 What's Included

- **Core Principles**: Hierarchical decomposition, near-decomposability, stable intermediate forms
- **Decision Framework**: Practical questions for architectural choices
- **Real Examples**: Vue vs React, MCP design, CAM framework
- **Full Theory**: Herbert Simon's 1962 paper included

## 📄 License

- Skill content: CC BY-SA 4.0
- CAM framework: Copyright © Jérémie Lumbroso
- Third X framework: Copyright © Jérémie Lumbroso

## 🔗 Resources

- Documentation: See README.md in the skill package
- Third X: https://thirdx.design
- GitHub: https://github.com/jlumbroso/architecture-of-complexity-skill

---

## Recent Changes

- docs: add repository README (e4ced6f)

---

**Authors**: Jérémie Lumbroso and Claude Sonnet 4.5
**Created**: February 2026
